===Chinese===

到九月二十五日英军再度攻击时，德军的士气正落到最低点。除右翼的博蒙阿梅尔和博库尔，他们已远远退出了七月一日前给他们安全感的那个庞大的掩体体系。他们第二、第三道防线已被夺去，正生活在无休止的炮火下仓促刨出的弹坑和堑壕里。

战场的恐怖堆到了苦痛与惊惧的顶点。活人住在不掩埋的死人中间，踏着成堆的尸体上前沿，呼吸着人肉腐烂的气味，耳边永远是救不回来的伤员的哭喊。他们把这些写进悲惨的信——成千上万封——大多没能到德国的家，躺在他们被夺走的沟里。

「四下躺着的死人，多得吓人。走两步就绊上一具。」

「围着我们躺的那些死人的臭气，忍无可忍。」

「我们在这里不再是人。我们比牲口还糟。」

「这是放出来的地狱。」……「太可怕了。」……「我们活在苦难里。」

「亲爱的家里人要是看见这一切，也许会有改变。可他们永远听不到。」

「大炮不停的吼要把我们逼疯了。」

可怜的、哀怜的信。从这些苦痛的哭喊里，人摸到了战争的真正真相——德国哲学家和英国主战派吹捧的那「光荣」与「壮丽」，把它捧为他们种族的大补剂、人的最高贵的经历。这些德国士兵写下的每一行，同样可能出自我们哪个人之手；在移动不定的战线两边，是同样的死亡、同样的地狱。

战线后方，德国总参谋部点数着一个个踉跄而出的虚弱的营和师的损失，用手边抓得到的预备队变戏法，把零散的建制一个个抛上去换堑壕里那些可怜虫。许多换防的部队上去时迷了路，来得迟了，又已被穿过的炮火打得七零八落。

「我们的阵地，」一个德国步兵军官写道，「同人家告诉我们的当然全然不同。光我们一个连就换了一个营的防。原先对我们说，我们换的是伤亡削弱到五十人的一个连。

===Original===

“The men we relieved had no idea where the enemy was, how far off he was, or whether any of our own troops were in front of us. We got no idea of our support position until six o’clock this evening. The English are four hundred yards away, by the windmill over the hill.”

One German soldier wrote that the British “seem to relieve their infantry very quickly, while the German commands work on the principle of relieving only in the direst need, and leaving the divisions in as long as possible.”

Another wrote that:

“The leadership of the divisions really fell through. For the most part we did not get orders, and the regiment had to manage as best it could. If orders arrived they generally came too late or were dealt out ‘from the green table’ without knowledge of the conditions in front, so that to carry them out was impossible.”

All this was a sign of demoralization, not only among the troops who were doing the fighting and the suffering, but among the organizing generals behind, who were directing the operations. The continual hammer-strokes of the British and French armies on the Somme battlefields strained the German war-machine on the western front almost to breaking-point.

It seemed as though a real debacle might happen, and that they would be forced to effect a general retreat﻿—a withdrawal more or less at ease or a retirement under pressure from the enemy.﻿ ﻿…

But they had luck﻿—astonishing luck. At the very time when the morale of the German soldiers was lowest and when the strain on the High Command was greatest the weather turned in their favor and gave them just the breathing-space they desperately needed. Rain fell heavily in the middle of October, autumn mists prevented airplane activity and artillery-work, and the ground became a quagmire, so that the British troops found it difficult to get up their supplies for a new advance.

The Germans were able in this respite to bring up new divisions, fresh and strong enough to make heavy counterattacks in the Stuff and Schwaben and Regina trenches, and to hold the lines more securely for a time, while great digging was done farther back at Bapaume and the next line of defense. Successive weeks of bad weather and our own tragic losses checked the impetus of the British and French driving power, and the Germans were able to reorganize and reform.

===Chinese===

「我们换下来的那伙人，不知道敌人在哪儿、有多远，也不知道我们前面还有没有自己人。到今晚六点，我们才弄清自己的支援位置。英格兰人就在四百码外，山那边风车磨旁边。」

一个德国士兵写道，英国人「似乎很快就给步兵换防，而德国的指挥信守的原则是：不到万不得已不换，部队在防线上留得越久越好」。

另一个写道：

「各师的指挥真的失灵了。多数时候我们接不到命令，团里只好自己想辙。命令来了，多半到得太迟，或者是『绿台子』后头發下来的，对前面的实情一无所知，根本没法执行。」

这一切都是瓦解的征候，不但在打仗受苦的部队里，也在后方指挥作战、组织调度的那群将军里。英法两军在索姆河战场连续的重锤，把西线的德国战争机器几乎压到了断裂点。

眼看一场真正的大溃败可能發生，他们将被逼作总退却——或从容或被敌人压着撤。……可他们的运气来了——惊人的运气。正当德军士气最低、最高统帅部压力最大的当口，天气转到了他们一边，给了他们急需的那一口喘息。十月中旬大雨滂沱，秋雾妨碍了飞行和炮兵作业，地面成了泥沼，英国部队难以运上發起新攻击的补给。

德国人趁着这个喘息调上新编的师，新鲜力壮，在斯图夫、施瓦本和雷吉娜堑壕發起猛烈反攻，把防线一时守得更牢；同时后方巴波姆和下一道防线在大力挖筑。接连数周的坏天气，加上我们自己的惨重损失，遏住了英法驱动力的势头，德国人得以重整旗鼓。

===Original===

As I have said, the shock of our offensive reached as far as Germany, and caused a complete reorganization in the system of obtaining reserves of manpower. The process of “combing out,” as we call it, was pursued with astounding ruthlessness, and German mothers, already stricken with the loss of their elder sons, raised cries of despair when the youngest born were also seized﻿—boys of eighteen belonging to the 1918 class.

The whole of the 1917 class had joined the depots in March and May of this year, receiving a three months’ training before being transferred to the field-recruit depots in June and July. About the middle of July the first large drafts joined their units and made their appearance at the front, and soon after the beginning of our offensive at least half this class was in the front-line regiments. The massacre of the boys had begun.

Then older men, men beyond middle age, who correspond to the French Territorial class, exempted from fighting service and kept on lines of communication, were also called to the front, and whole garrisons of these gray heads were removed from German towns to fill up the ranks.

“The view is held here,” wrote a German soldier of the Somme, “that the Higher Command intends gradually to have more and more Landsturm battalions (men of the oldest reserves) trained in trench warfare for a few weeks, as we have been, according to the quality of the men, and thus to secure by degrees a body of troops on which it can count in an emergency.”

In the month of November the German High Command believed that the British attacks were definitely at an end, “having broken down,” as they claimed, “in mud and blood,” but another shock came to them when once more British troops﻿—the 51st Highland Division and the 63rd Naval Division﻿—left their trenches, in fog and snow, and captured the strongest fortress position on the enemy’s front, at Beaumont Hamel, bringing back over six thousand prisoners. It was after that they began their retreat.

===Chinese===

如我所说，我们攻势的震动直抵德国本土，引起了人力预备获取体制的彻底重组。我们所谓「梳笔」的那道工序，以骇人的冷酷推行；德国的母亲们——大儿子们已经折了——见最小的孩子也被抓走，發出绝望的哭喊：一九一八级的十八岁男孩子们。

一九一七级的全体已于本年三、五月补入兵站，受三个月训练，六、七月转入前线新兵站。七月中旬，第一批大宗补员加入各自部队、出现在前线；我们攻势开始后不久，这一级至少有一半已在前沿各团里。对少年的屠杀开始了。

接着是更老的人，超过中年的、相当于法国本土卫戍级的那批人——原免兵役、留在通讯线上——也被征上前线，整队整队的白发守备从德国城镇里拔出来填队伍。

「这里流行一种看法，」索姆河一个德国士兵写道，「最高统帅部打算逐步让更多的后备军营（最老的预备役）按人的素质受几个星期堑壕战训练——像我们受过的那样——从而渐渐攒出一支紧急时靠得住的部队。」

十一月里，德国最高统帅部相信英军的攻击已确定告终——他们声称「陷在泥血里垮掉了」——可又挨了一记震：英国部队——第51高地师和第63海军师——在雾和雪里再次跃出堑壕，攻下了敌军前线最坚固的堡垒阵地博蒙阿梅尔，押回六千多名俘虏。那之后，他们才开始撤退。

===Original===

These studies of mine, of what happened on both sides of the shifting lines in the Somme, must be as horrible to read as they were to write. But they are less than the actual truth, for no pen will ever in one book, or in hundreds, give the full record of the individual agony, the broken heart-springs, the soul-shock as well as the shell-shock, of that frightful struggle in which, on one side and the other, two million men were engulfed. Modern civilization was wrecked on those fire-blasted fields, though they led to what we called “Victory.” More died there than the flower of our youth and German manhood. The Old Order of the world died there, because many men who came alive out of that conflict were changed, and vowed not to tolerate a system of thought which had led up to such a monstrous massacre of human beings who prayed to the same God, loved the same joys of life, and had no hatred of one another except as it had been lighted and inflamed by their governors, their philosophers, and their newspapers. The German soldier cursed the militarism which had plunged him into that horror. The British soldier cursed the German as the direct cause of all his trouble, but looked back on his side of the lines and saw an evil there which was also his enemy﻿—the evil of a secret diplomacy which juggled with the lives of humble men so that war might be sprung upon them without their knowledge or consent, and the evil of rulers who hated German militarism not because of its wickedness, but because of its strength in rivalry and the evil of a folly in the minds of men which had taught them to regard war as a glorious adventure, and patriotism as the right to dominate other peoples, and liberty as a catchword of politicians in search of power. After the Somme battles there were many other battles as bloody and terrible, but they only confirmed greater numbers of men in the faith that the old world had been wrong in its “makeup” and wrong in its religion of life. Lip service to Christian ethics was not good enough as an argument for this. Either the heart of the world must be changed by a real obedience to the gospel of Christ or Christianity must be abandoned for a new creed which would give better results between men and nations. There could be no reconciling of bayonet-drill and high explosives with the words “Love one another.”

===Chinese===

我这些关于索姆河移动战线两侧所见的研究，写它们的人觉得可怕，读它们的人也必觉得可怕。可它们还不及实际的真相，因为无论一支笔写一本书、还是写几百本，都永远写不尽那场可怕争斗里个人的苦痛、断裂的心簧、灵魂的震荡连同炮弹的震荡——在那场争斗里，两边合计两百万人被吞没。现代文明就搁浅在那些火燎的田野上，尽管它们通向了我们所谓的「胜利」。在那里死去的，不止是我们青年的精华和德国的男子气概。世界的旧秩序死在了那里，因为从那场冲突里活着出来的许多人变了，發誓不再容忍那样一种思想体系——它导致了这样一场对人类的大屠杀，而这些人向同一位上帝祷告，爱同样的人生欢乐，彼此之间本无仇恨，除了被他们的统治者、他们的哲学家和他们的报纸点起来、煽起来的那份。德国士兵咒骂把他掷进那恐怖的军国主义。英国士兵咒骂德国人，把他当作自己一切苦难的直接根源；可他回望自己这一侧的战线，看见那里也有一种恶，同样是他敌人的——秘密外交的恶，它拿卑微人的性命变戏法，好叫战争不经他们的知晓与同意就落到他们头上；统治者的恶，他们恨德国军国主义，不是恨它的恶，而是恨它竞争中的强；还有一种蠢的恶，在人心里教他们把战争当光荣的奇遇、把爱国主义当统治他族的权利、把自由当政客猎取权力的口头禅。索姆河诸役之后还有许多别的同样血腥可怕的战役，可它们只是叫更多的人坚定了这个信念：旧世界错了它的「构造」，也错了它的人生宗教。对基督教伦理的口惠，不足以为此辩护。要么世界的心必须靠真正顺服基督的福音而改变，要么基督教就必须被抛弃，换一个能在人与国之间给出更好结果的新信条。刺枪教练和高爆炸药，同「彼此相爱」那句话之间，是无法调和的。

===Original===

Or if bayonet-drill and high-explosive force were to be the rule of life in preparation for another struggle such as this, then at least let men put hypocrisy away and return to the primitive law of the survival of the fittest in a jungle world subservient to the king of beasts. The devotion of military chaplains to the wounded, their valor, their decorations for gallantry under fire, their human comradeship and spiritual sincerity, would not bridge the gulf in the minds of many soldiers between a gospel of love and this argument by bayonet and bomb, gas-shell and high velocity, blunderbuss, club, and trench-shovel. Some time or other, when German militarism acknowledged defeat by the break of its machine or by the revolt of its people﻿—not until then﻿—there must be a new order of things, which would prevent such another massacre in the fair fields of life, and that could come only by a faith in the hearts of many peoples breaking down old barriers of hatred and reaching out to one another in a fellowship of common sense based on common interests, and inspired by an ideal higher than this beast-like rivalry of nations. So thinking men thought and talked. So said the soldier-poets who wrote from the trenches. So said many onlookers. The simple soldier did not talk like that unless he were a Frenchman. Our men only began to talk like that after the war﻿—as many of them are now talking﻿—and the revolt of the spirit, vague but passionate, against the evil that had produced this devil’s trap of war, and the German challenge, was subconscious as they sat in their dugouts and crowded in their ditches in the battles of the Somme.

===Chinese===

倘若刺枪教练和高爆炸药的暴力就是要成为生活的法则，作为另一场这样的厮杀的准备，那么至少让人丢掉伪善，回到那条原始的法则——适者生存，回到臣服于兽王的丛林世界。随军牧师们对伤员的奉献、他们的英勇、炮火下的受勋、他们人间的情谊和心魂的诚恳，也弥合不了许多士兵心里那条沟：一边是爱的福音，一边是这刺刀与炸弹、毒气弹与高速炮、大口铳、枪托和堑壕铁锹的论证。或早或迟——当德国军国主义以其机器的断裂或其人民的反抗认输的时候——不到那时不算——必须有一种事物的新秩序，防止人生的好田野上再来这样一场屠杀；而那只能靠许多民族心里的信义，推倒仇恨的旧墙，在共同利益之上、怀着比这种兽性的民族角逐更高的理想，伸手彼此相认。思考的人这样想、这样说。从堑壕里写作的士兵诗人这样说。许多旁观者也这样说。单纯的士兵不这样说——除非他是法国人。我们的人是打完仗之后才开始这样说的——如今他们中许多人还在说——而在索姆河诸役里，当他们坐在掩体中、挤在堑壕里时，那种精神上的反叛——模糊而炽热，对生出这个战争魔鬼陷阱的恶、对德国的挑衅的反叛——还在他们的潜意识里。
